- MM II is consistent with MM I; if the benefits of greater use of lower-cost debt financing are just offset by the increased cost of equity, the proportions of debt versus equity in the firm’s capital structure do not affect the firm’s overall cost of capital or the value of the firm. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kp5ve529td5n9g9yf9bw6yq1))
- MM With Taxes: Value Is Maximized at 100% Debt ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kp5vecnbvmbea7se8er5asxj))
- Here, we examine the MM propositions under the assumptions that earnings are taxed and that interest payments to debtholders are tax deductible. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kp8xgrzhqn3at90pj62qe5aj))
- Under the tax code of most countries, interest payments are a pretax expense and are therefore tax deductible, while dividends paid to equity holders are not tax deductible. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kp8xh2f5trzcw695x00wd271))
- This differential tax treatment encourages firms to use debt financing because debt provides a tax shield that adds value to the company ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kp8y102mppj3fxv6t7jgrp40))
    - Note: and it just so happens to also inject liquidity back into the market, seems like a win win 
      i guess depends on your leanings
- The tax shield is equal to the tax rate multiplied by the amount of debt in the capital structure ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kp8y24s8c447jm3txd9ascb6))
- In other words, the value of a levered firm is equal to the value of an unlevered firm plus the value of the tax shield provided by debt financing. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kp8y26yvzpy12tp0a054njph))
    - Note: the additional benefit you get from the tax you dont have to pya is pretty large and needs to be acknowledged I guess
- To continue our analogy of a pie, with the introduction of taxes, the government gets a slice of the pie. When debt financing is used, the government’s slice of the pie is smaller, so that the amount of pie available to debt and equity holders is greater. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprak898d7ejxyay57g5yfmm))
- The use of debt reduces taxes due to deductibility of interest expense giving rise to debt tax shield. If a firm were 100% financed with debt, the taxes avoided would be at a maximum, and the after-tax cash flows of the firm (size of the pie) would be maximized. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprakr0ysa6fbw7v6k0pbww2))
- ![](https://readwise-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/media/reader/pub/94bb301a7a5b5db718eb15868c85d99b.png) ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kpram990qyypz1p0jm75zjme))
- costs of financial distress, ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprbkd98s180exrnmty4x98c))
- Costs of financial distress are the increased costs a company faces when earnings decline to the point where the firm has trouble paying its fixed financing costs (interest on debt). ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprbkjedzg31kh3zycj6zfw7))
- 1. Costs of financial distress and bankruptcy can be direct or indirect. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprc11c4sxkabh7hvxd8kq9c))
- Direct costs of financial distress include the cash expenses associated with the bankruptcy, such as legal fees and administrative fees. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprc13g13vy5bd0qty7t2bwv))
- Indirect costs include foregone investment opportunities and the costs that result from losing the trust of customers, creditors, suppliers, and employees. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprc183ss4ghj0q7w7w21s20))
- Additionally, during periods of financial distress, conflicts of interest between managers (who represent equity owners) and debtholders impose additional costs, referred to as the agency costs of debt ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprc1ftt57mem5235dst82ck))
    - Note: the cost of having debt is called the agency cost of debt
- 2. The probability of financial distress is related to the firm’s use of operating and financial leverage ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprcaz22cr098wnrzab6xjdr))
- In general, higher amounts of financial leverage increase the probability of financial distress ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprcbfnmdzehchy65j4s6bt1))
    - Note: since there is more downside risk here essentially. since your fixed costs are soo much higher, that yourm amrgin for error is lower 
      if you have stock and your cashflows fall then, worst comes to worst your stock value decreases, thats fine as you live another day, but with high debt you dont have the option to live another day
- Other factors to consider include the quality of a firm’s management and the company’s corporate governance structure. Lower-quality management and poor corporate governance lead to a higher probability of financial distress. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprce1zk9fhrhx8efem72ps4))
- Static Tradeoff Theory ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprcqmn4zv8nfr4n5ncrmbkv))
- The static tradeoff theory seeks to balance the costs of financial distress with the tax shield benefits from using debt. There is an amount of debt financing at which the increase in the value of the tax shield from additional borrowing is exceeded by the value reduction of higher expected costs of financial distress. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprcs9036sqk4pdxsf1gqzkq))
- optimal capital structure for a firm, where the WACC is minimized and the value of the firm is maximized. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprcsjbrnv38y6fbherb0ta7))

- MM’s propositions with no taxes or costs of financial distress are that a company’s capital structure is irrelevant because its WACC and its value (the discounted present value of its operating earnings) are unchanged by changes in capital structure. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprczs25zpwqfsnpjjbpp2a9))
- MM’s propositions with taxes but without costs of financial distress are that a company’s WACC is minimized and its value is maximized with 100% debt. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprczwrvgata91q4r9gr87hr))
- Static tradeoff theory indicates that firm value initially increases (and WACC decreases) with additional debt financing, but company value decreases at some point when the increase in the expected value of financial distress outweighs the tax benefits of additional debt. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd043hr6jjb5r7j312a1tb))
- Target capital structure is the capital structure that a firm seeks to achieve on average over time to maximize firm value ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd136y9c4pbc0wr6qr3qn1))
- . That is, it reflects management’s beliefs about its optimal capital structure as well as other internal and external factors. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd1a8ybqyahg1wvpyxcdc3))
- For analysis, the weights to use when estimating a firm’s WACC should be based on its target capital structure ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd64hv9dxghhs98jcf1pm9))
    - Note: target capital structure can be estimated vs its peers
- the proportions (based on market values) of debt, preferred stock, and equity that the firm expects to achieve over time. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd66m9r3ww2tq3pe2namn6))
- An analyst may use management’s stated target proportions of equity and debt; however, most firms do not provide these. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd74mfsc60dgybtzd2xj8g))
- In the absence of stated capital structure weights, an external analyst must estimate a firm’s target capital structure. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd7n2ckss5n17h8e9v21ak))
- An analyst may simply use the firm’s current capital structure (based on market values) as the best indication of its target capital structure. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd7qpfbnfdsmjyq3fqxvy5))
- If there has been a noticeable trend in the firm’s capital structure, an analyst may incorporate this trend into her estimate of the firm’s target. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd7x3cb0ebm7a432f0nd8a))
- An analyst may use the average capital structure weights for a firm’s industry ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd811py22s8xzgg6t3exbw))
- In contrast with independent analysts, company managers often focus on book values of debt and equity. Reasons book values may be appropriate for internal analysis include: ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd8d6a3h7vbfa3fsdw043n))
- Short-term fluctuations in the market values of debt and equity do not really affect a company’s appropriate level of debt. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd8gswrnav6pvb73p0enm2))
- While market values reflect the perspectives of investors in a company, management’s perspective has more to do with how the company needs to deploy its capital to projects. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd8qheva5a0tt14bveqajw))
- Credit rating agencies use book values in their measures of credit quality. Managers may make capital structure decisions with these measures in mind ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd90bxhdefxcax3q9m3dvk))
- a firm’s actual capital structure tends to fluctuate around the target capital structure. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd9czfgabxqtzgmm0v523j))
- Market fluctuations, especially in the market value of a firm’s equity, may cause the firm’s capital structure to vary from the target ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd9frsnf8ecyxjkez4makp))
- Management may choose to exploit opportunities in a specific financing source. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd9tv6e9n0wzd58qbc095z))
- For example, a temporary rise in the firm’s stock price may create a good opportunity to issue additional equity, which would result in a higher percentage of equity than the target ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprd9zbwv9n9wapcgzxg718e))
- Also, external capital is raised in minimum-size lots; hence, adherence to precise weights may not be possible ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprda68zewvfdvr6tg11mb12))
- Costs of asymmetric information arise from the fact that managers typically have more information about a company’s prospects and future performance than owners or creditors ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprdavr2yywyg1mq3sccqcnk))
- Firms with complex products or little transparency in financial statements tend to have higher costs of asymmetric information, which result in higher required returns on both debt and equity capital. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprdb09zw7svd9r69407rge2))
    - Note: if you are hiding more, people are taking much more risk, so you need to pay money to adjust for this risk

- 1. Monitoring costs are associated with supervising management and include the expenses of reporting to shareholders and paying the board of directors. Strong corporate governance systems reduce monitoring costs. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprdxge564jf81d6ytkbs3t4))

- According to the free cash flow hypothesis, the use of debt forces managers to be disciplined with regard to how they spend cash because they have less free cash flow to use for their own benefit. It follows that greater amounts of financial leverage tend to reduce agency costs. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprdzb6pv8gw97d15nhc6z5a))
- Pecking order theory, based on asymmetric information, is related to the signals that management sends to investors through its financing choices ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprdzqedkhh0hway1fch0yd7))
- According to pecking order theory, managers prefer to make financing choices that are least likely to send negative signals to investors ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprdzxjfb59qkwb7f51vn42t))
- Financing choices under pecking order theory follow a hierarchy based on visibility to investors. - Pecking order theory implies that the capital structure is a by- product of individual financing decisions. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kpre0m336ebz3b452n4cyyee))

- A business model explains how a firm ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfssrt2tmgq91352x8an6p))
    - Note: provides a product or a service, find customers, deliver the prouct or service and make a profit. 
      builidn gon the definition of the business model
- “How we will provide it, sell it, and make a profit” is clearly an oversimplification, but this is the essence of a business model ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfxjbvt1kpaf8xggmd2vgg))
- Here, we present a framework that incorporates some of the complexities involved. It involves answering the questions of who, what, how, where, and how much. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfxqqq8ngf87f24qq0jx2k))
- who, what, how, where, and how much ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprgdd9ernasmkav810k8ba4))
- A business model should do the following: ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfxts4esp98777yxamxjpt))
- Identify the firm’s potential customers (who), how they are acquired, the cost of customer acquisition, and how the company will monitor and maintain customer satisfaction. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfy4x7db47g41fhe7y6tf3))
- Potential customers can be defined in innumerable ways, ranging from every consumer within a geographic area, to dog owners, to only the home country’s military in the case of a weapon ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfyahn6w0y6gtva5rjcw3k))
- This characterization of customer groups is called segmenting. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfyh9ypdfvxh53y4k39sek))
    - Note: creating segments of possible customers
- Describe the key assets and suppliers of the firm (how) ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfz0hhy6xqpqprknzmptfk))
- Key assets may be, for example, a patent, software, or skilled employees. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfz3p8xbd5m01qhn3gc4d8))
- Key suppliers may be a battery manufacturer for an electric vehicle company, a lithium miner for a battery maker, or a supplier of large excavation machinery for a lithium miner. ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprfz7x896rep1zp7e2r8z29))
- Describe the firm’s product or service (what), how it meets a need for its potential customers, and what differentiates its products from those of competitors (e.g., low price, premium quality, innovative features). ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprg77yqxyvq3b4s2rzdxxsf))
- Explain how the firm will sell its product or service (where), such as online, physical location, direct mail, trade shows, or through sales representatives; ([View Highlight](https://read.readwise.io/read/01kprg7mjk19kb5p341sw2xcp1))
